| H8 | Excluding advance/mobile/special ballots (~47% of 2023 votes; reported only at ED level, not VA level) materially biases the audit's Lane-1 metrics | **REJECTED — empirical refutation** | Apportioning the advance/mobile/special ballots back to VAs (`analysis/scripts/advance_vote_splat.py`, commit `72e3369`) shifts both v0_9 maps by **exactly +1.12 pp** in `seats@50/50` (majority 0.4607 → 0.4719, minority 0.4831 → 0.4944). The 2.25-pp inter-map gap is preserved to 4 decimal places. The apportionment keeps each poll group's actual party split (one group per 2019 district); election-day two-party votes only set how a group's ballots are spread across its VAs. This run predates the Vote Anywhere exclusion, so it placed all 1,706,304 two-party votes (UCP 54.4%). The current apportioned-ballot substrate holds 1.54M of them (Vote Anywhere excluded; UCP 55.3%). The advance-vote omission is a real methodological caveat (every map gains 1.12pp UCP under the smear) but is **inter-map invariant** at the audit's percentile-comparison resolution. The hostile-witness attack ("you're missing half the electorate") is empirically refuted as a methodological objection. Side observation (v0_9 substrate): the majority's efficiency gap flipped sign (−0.0149) under the smear while the minority's barely moved (+0.0170 vs +0.0175); on the canonical substrate neither flips (majority +0.10 % → +0.52 %, minority +4.02 % → +5.79 %; [§5.2.1](#sec-5-2-1)) | `findings/advance_vote_sensitivity.md`; `data/v0_9_advance_vote_*.json`; `analysis/methodology/methodological_defenses.md` §1.1 |
| H9 | The targeted hill-climbing procedure used to bound the minority's distance from neutral is asymmetric (UCP-direction works, NDP-direction does not, so the comparison with neutral median is rigged) | **REJECTED — symmetric** | NDP-maximizing short-bursts run on the v0_9 substrate (`analysis/scripts/targeted_gerrymander_burst_ndp.py`, mirror of UCP-direction config; commit `72e3369`). UCP-targeted reaches 52.87% UCP `seats@50/50`; NDP-targeted reaches 37.93%. Neutral 250k v0_9 ensemble bounds are 39.08%–50.57%. Both targeted procedures push beyond the neutral envelope (UCP +2.30pp above max, NDP −1.15pp below min). Asymmetry in degree (UCP has 8.04pp targeted headroom from neutral median; NDP has 6.90pp) is a property of Alberta's political geography, not the test apparatus. The minority sits 2.3× closer to the UCP-targeted ceiling than to the NDP-targeted floor — the audit's existing framing holds. **Side correction:** `report_public.md:333` lists "Neutral 250k MCMC, min produced = 37.9%" — the value is in fact the NDP-burst result, not the neutral floor. Corrected neutral floor on the canonical v0_9 250k ensemble is 39.08% | `findings/burst_symmetry_analysis.md`; `data/v0_9_ndp_burst_*.json` |
| H10 | The "Polsby-Popper says the commission majority's named lasso (Calgary-Nolan Hill-Cochrane) is moderately compact" finding (PP = 0.402, v0_9) reflects the actual geometry of the lasso. If a different but equally well-known compactness metric (Reock — pre-registered alongside PP in the audit's protocol) also scores this district as moderate-or-better, then the lasso shape claim must rest entirely on the majority's judgement and not on any mathematical compactness metric | **REJECTED on v0_9 — Reock caught it; reversed on the official boundaries** | Reock = 4·area/(π·D²) where D is the diameter of the smallest enclosing circle. Computed on v0_9 in commit `e219943` (`analysis/scripts/reock.py`). Calgary-Nolan Hill-Cochrane scores Reock = **0.230** — *flagged* under the conventional Reock < 0.30 threshold. The two metrics measure different aspects of compactness: PP penalises perimeter wiggle, Reock penalises elongation. A lasso corridor is exactly the elongation pattern Reock catches and PP does not. The H3 conclusion — "compactness is not the mechanism for the *seats@50/50* gap" — still holds (the falsification test was on plan-level mean PP, and the v0_9 majority and minority have similar mean Reock too), but the *individual lasso shape* was quantitatively flagged by Reock on v0_9. On the official boundaries the result reverses: Reock is 0.357 (not flagged) and PP 0.236 (borderline) (`findings/reock_verdict.md`, `findings/polsby_popper_verdict.md`). Both pre-registered metrics now in the record | `findings/reock_verdict.md`; `data/reock_per_district.csv` |

**State of the audit's central claim after H1–H10.** The audit's pre-shapefile Lane 2 finding — the minority municipal anchoring dropped from the 2019 baseline of 75.1 % to 14.5 % while the majority continued 2019 practice at 71.0 % — was the central draft claim through April 2026 (never published) and survived every DPG-era methodological revision (sampler, swing, compactness, ReCom population tolerance). It did **not** survive canonical recomputation against the official 2026 shapefiles: minority 71.8 %, majority 80.0 %, both close to the 2019 map's 75.1 %.[^anchoring_canonical] H1–H10 are preserved here as the audit's historical stress-test record; the H4 row in particular references the DPG-era anchoring values not surviving canonical recomputation. H5's canonical regional-swing recomputation **substantially weakens but does not collapse** the minority's seats@50/50 finding: under canonical EA shapefiles and election-day votes (MCMC-consistent substrate), the minority's regional-swing value (41 of 89) ranks at p78.54 against the uniform-swing ensemble, and ties the maximum of a 10,000-plan regional-swing ensemble once district counts are allowed for ([§5.2.2](#sec-5-2-2)) — extreme tail on the like-for-like comparison, not beyond it. The DPG-era "finding relocates to majority at p99.5" result does not replicate on canonical geometry; the majority instead drifts toward the NDP-tail or ensemble median. H6's corrected 1,010,000-plan canonical ensemble retains the headline at p99.97 on mean-median and p99.99 on seats@50/50. Lane 1 is therefore reported as a multi-scenario sensitivity table (uniform swing / regional swing × two vote substrates / cross-election direction holds 3-of-3). The audit's central published finding has reorganized: the MCMC ensemble (parametric Mahalanobis p = 8.80×10⁻⁷ against the 1,010,000-plan canonical ensemble; 89-district maps against 87-district plans, size-matched test pending) is now the load-bearing Lane 1 result, beside the four geometry-independent Lane 2 dimensions (population dispersion, Calgary zone, Airdrie split, majority-flagged anomalies), which compare the two maps.

**Canonical placements and the asymmetric-packing reading.** Two findings from the H6 disposition stand out for the audit's framing:

1. **The 2019 enacted map and the 2026 majority sit at *identical* percentile on `seats@50/50`** (both p78.54 against the canonical 1,010,000-plan ensemble). The majority continues 2019 Alberta practice on the partisan-fairness axis. On canonical geometry the majority's municipal anchoring (80.0 %) also sits in the 2019 baseline neighbourhood (75.1 %); the minority's canonical anchoring (71.8 %) sits slightly below it.[^anchoring_canonical] The partisan-fairness continuation framing — the majority continues 2019 Alberta practice, the minority diverges on the metrics surviving canonical recomputation — remains supported.

2. **The minority's Lane-1 metric pattern, against the canonical 1,010,000-plan ensemble, is three tail flags and one near-but-below — all four directionally UCP-favoured (declination sign correction of Amendment 5, Part 9 applied 2026-06-12).** Mean-median p99.97 (UCP-tail), **declination p98.79 (UCP-tail under the corrected Warrington 2018 convention; earlier drafts cited p1.21 NDP-tail under the swapped-operand implementation)**, and seats@50/50 p99.99 are the three flags; efficiency gap at p94.54 sits *near, but below*, the audit's pre-registered Alberta-calibrated p95 / 4.10 % threshold (directionally UCP-favoured but sub-threshold). (An earlier draft of this paragraph cited "near-median" Lane-1 placements based on the v0_9 ensemble; those numbers — EG p56.1, MM p53.2, declination p62.2 — are superseded by the canonical 1,010,000-plan placements.) The pattern of three tail flags plus a near-miss on EG is consistent with asymmetric packing: tight NDP winning margins (declination), over-concentration of NDP support in high-share districts (MM), and a seat ceiling reached well below 50 % UCP vote share (seats@50/50). EG measures average wasted-vote efficiency, which a pattern concentrated in a small number of marginal districts can leave only weakly perturbed; that may be why EG sits just below threshold while the other three tail metrics fire.

The DPG-substrate SMC cross-validation had a seed-placement defect: three runs with the same nominal `set.seed(88)` produced 5.6%, 28%, and 58% of weighted plans reaching the DPG minority's 0.4831 value, because `library(redistmetrics)` consumed RNG state before the sampler ran. The defect was diagnosed and corrected by placing `set.seed(852751799)` immediately before `redist_smc()`. The canonical re-run (2026-05-18; input: `va_2023_election_day_votes.gpkg`; 5,000 SMC plans, ESS 1,116) corroborates the Python ReCom finding: **0% of 5,000 SMC plans reached 0.5169** (ensemble maximum 0.4943, placing the canonical minority map above the 99.98th SMC percentile). Both standard samplers on official Elections Alberta geometry agree: the canonical minority map's `seats@50/50` exceeds the ensemble tail. The Python ReCom ensemble retains priority for percentile placement — it passes the Gelman-Rubin diagnostic (R̂ ∈ [1.00011, 1.00205] across all four metrics, below the 1.05 publication threshold) and has 200× the sample size — but the canonical SMC result now provides algorithm-independence corroboration rather than a contradicting signal. (The H3 falsification test was run on the v0_9 DPG substrate against the 0.4831 threshold; on canonical geometry the threshold is never reached, making the PP comparison of high-vs-low plans moot. The direction of H3 — high-UCP-advantage plans more compact, not less — is preserved: the stronger statement now is no SMC plan reaches the canonical target at all.)

<a id="sec-7-1"></a>
### 7.1 Missing evidence and scope limits


1. **2026 polygon geometry.** Phase 4A resolved 2026-05-06 (canonical EA shapefiles received; MCMC canonical ensemble complete). C1 (Polsby-Popper, 2026-05-18) and C2 (Reock, 2026-05-18) executed on canonical EA shapefiles — null findings; v0_9 direction reversed (documented in DOCUMENTED CORRECTIONS). B5 (MCMC seed variant) pending.
2. **Hybrid-ED blend vs. spatial measurement disagreement (Phase 4C vs. v0.2).** Phase 4C spatial assignment on **canonical EA shapefiles** (official Elections Alberta geometry, re-run 2026-05-17; `phase4c_canonical_attribution.py`) produces a **+3.92 pp** EG asymmetry (election-day votes; majority NDP@50/50 = 48, minority = 43). An earlier Phase 4C v2 run using derived shapefiles had produced +0.31 pp — a smaller figure attributable to derived boundary approximation of rural-fringe VAs. v0.2's 70/30 hybrid-ED blend on full-vote totals produces −1.41 pp (reversed sign). H8's empirical test shows advance-ballot addition shifts both maps by an identical +1.12 pp (inter-map invariant), confirming the method-vs-blend tension is not a vote-data gap. Full reconciliation: `findings/assignment_gerrymander_comparison.md`.
3. **Independent verification of the no-public-support claim (Section D).** Done ([§5.9.4](#sec-5-9-4)): text search of 1,252 of the 1,345 submissions; about 93 image-only submissions remain unsearched.
4. **Full-symmetry visual audit for majority.** Done: all eight majority Appendix A panels were inspected ([§5.8.1](#sec-5-8-1)); the remaining limit is that the anomaly questions were built from the minority's boundaries ([§5.8.3](#sec-5-8-3)).
5. **2019-era population data.** Done for A1: Appendix C scores the 2019 map on 2021 census counts beside the two 2026 proposals. A2 has not been run on the 2019 map.
6. **Multiple testing (family-wise error rate).** Under a 4-comparison Bonferroni correction for the minority's four partisan-bias metrics (threshold p98.75), mean-median (p99.97) and seats@50/50 (p99.99) survive, declination (p98.79) sits at the boundary, and the efficiency gap (p94.54) does not ([§5.2.1](#sec-5-2-1)). The paper does not otherwise apply a family-wise correction ([§6](#sec-6)).


<a id="sec-7-2"></a>
### 7.2 Falsifiability statement


The audit's directional claim — *minority more UCP-favorable than majority across population, spatial, partisan-bias, and procedural dimensions* — would be falsified by any of the following:

- An alternative Calgary classification producing near-null minority-majority asymmetry (≤1%) while A2's current rule produces >10%. Tested; both rules produce the same direction.

- Submission-archive evidence the five disputed minority configurations (Airdrie, Cochrane, Chestermere, Red Deer, St. Albert) did have substantial public support in the 1,345-submission record. Refutes Section D claim.
- Visible spatial anomalies in the majority's rural or Edmonton districts of a severity comparable to the minority's three confirmed ridings. All majority panels have been inspected with the minority-derived question set ([§5.8.3](#sec-5-8-3)); a question set built for the majority's possible anomaly classes has not been run.
- A comprehensive survey of Canadian provincial redistributions 1991–2025 finding comparable mid-cycle government-drafting-process replacements. Refutes Section D uniqueness framing.

<a id="sec-7-3"></a>
### 7.3 Academic Limitations and Methodological Caveats

1. **Ecological Inference:** We use 2023 election results to simulate hypothetical 50/50 vote splits. Actual 2026 vote distributions at the block-level may differ structurally from the uniform-swing projections.
2. **Single Election Dependency:** The 2023 election was highly competitive (UCP 52.6%, NDP 44.0%). In a landslide environment, structural partisan metrics become noisy or uninformative.
3. **Geographic Constraints vs Intent:** Alberta's deep urban/rural divide creates severe natural packing. The "neutral" ReCom ensemble attempts to control for this, but residual natural packing may still be misattributed as intentional engineering (Chen and Rodden 2013).
4. **Model Dependence:** ReCom ensembles assume contiguous, population-balanced districts as the primary constraints. If the commission heavily weighted unmeasured criteria (e.g., historical communities of interest not aligned with municipal boundaries), the neutral baseline may be misspecified.
5. **Causal Identification:** The audit mathematically detects structural advantage, not human intent. The commission may have had legitimate, non-partisan reasons for their boundary choices happening to perfectly correlate with partisan advantage.

> <a id="documented-corrections-c10"></a>**C10 — 2026-07-12 ensemble scrapped and rerun clean (supersedes C9).** Rather than continue publishing on the disclosed chain-1-duplicated run, the canonical ensemble was discarded and regenerated from scratch after a second, independent defect was found in the same code path: `mcmc_ensemble_canonical.py`'s multi-chain checkpoint writer indexed output files by loop position rather than by `--first-chain-idx`-adjusted chain number, which could clobber one chain's samples with another's on a partial or resumed run (hardened the same day as the chunked-resume fix described in C9). Every canonical-ensemble-derived number throughout both reports and the viewer site was updated to the new run's values on 2026-07-12. C9's "retained as-is" disposition applied to the disclosed duplication only; on finding a second, independent defect in the same code path, the audit's judgment was that a full clean rerun was owed rather than a second sensitivity footnote. > **C11 — 2026-07-12 blend-model Monte Carlo check retired; replaced by attribution-method sensitivity.** `analysis/scripts/monte_carlo_ci.py` — the source of the [§1.2](#sec-1-2) item 1 / [§5.2.3](#sec-5-2-3) / RT1 ([§5.7](#sec-5-7)) figures (90.5% direction consistency, CI [−3.04, +0.76] pp) — was found to have been unable to run since the v0.2 blend model it tests was retired 2026-05-18 (DOCUMENTED CORRECTIONS C5): its import of the blend model's `estimate_2026` function broke when that function was removed in favour of Phase 4C's exact VA-level spatial attribution. The script had gone unnoticed as broken because nothing had tried to rerun it since the retirement, and its hardcoded output values (never persisted to a file) had simply stayed in the report text. Two problems, not one: the check was unreproducible, and — independent of that — it was quantifying uncertainty in a model the audit itself no longer uses for the headline point estimate, while the actual estimate (Phase 4C, +3.92 pp) sits well outside the blend model's own interval, a mismatch the report had not previously reconciled. Persistence was added to `monte_carlo_ci.py` regardless (for provenance, should the blend model ever need re-examination), but it is not being resurrected: reconstructing a retired model's parameter uncertainty to caveat a claim no longer based on that model would not have improved accuracy. In its place, the audit now cites attribution-*method* sensitivity as the applicable robustness check for Phase 4C's exact figures: centroid-in-polygon (the audit's method) and population-weighted MAUP (the most rigorous attribution available without external polling-station geocoding) agree to within 0.1 pp on every partisan-bias metric on both maps; the minority's headline EG varies by at most 0.07 pp across all three tested methods (`findings/maup_attribution_canonical.md`, verified reproducible 2026-07-12). This is a different *kind* of robustness evidence — method agreement on an exact computation, not a sampling-based confidence interval — and is described as such everywhere it now appears. The retired Monte Carlo figures are preserved as historical record at each citing location rather than deleted. All citing passages ([§1.2](#sec-1-2), [§5.2.2](#sec-5-2-2), [§5.2.3](#sec-5-2-3), [§5.2.4](#sec-5-2-4), [§5.7](#sec-5-7) RT1, [§4.3.1](#sec-4-3-1) multiple-comparison posture, closing scope-discipline argument) were updated 2026-07-12.

> **C12 — 2026-07-13 municipal-splits analysis was never computed on canonical geometry; fixed, then re-fixed after a threshold-calibration error.** [§5.8](#sec-5-8)'s "Spatial CSD fragmentation analysis" (Strathcona County 3→10 EDs under the minority, "the headline finding... concentrated in a single dramatic case") traced to `analysis/scripts/municipal_splits.py`, whose `Backward:` header already pointed at the canonical `ea_majority_2026_eds.gpkg`/`ea_minority_2026_eds.gpkg` shapefiles — but whose `count_splits()` function hardcoded a lookup on the column name `name_2026`, which the canonical shapefiles do not have (they use `EDName2025`). The script had raised a `KeyError` on every run since the 2026-05-06 canonical-shapefile switch; nothing had rerun it since, so the report was silently citing its last successful run — 2026-04-27, before official shapefiles existed. Fixing the column-name mismatch (the same fix already applied to `cross_election.py` earlier this session) let the script run, but produced an even less plausible result on the first pass: majority 23 splits (+13), minority 29 (+19), Strathcona 8/7. Before publishing that number, a spot check of Strathcona's actual intersection geometry found the cause: the script's 5-hectare (50,000 m²) absolute-overlap threshold — calibrated against smoothed DPG-era boundaries during the audit's pre-shapefile phase — does not filter boundary-following slivers in precise official geometry. Five of Strathcona's eight "hits" under the majority map covered between 0.001% and 0.013% of the municipality's area (0.76–15.77 ha out of 121,623 ha); the three genuine splits covered 5.6%, 33.2%, and 61.1%. The threshold was changed to require both ≥ 50 ha *and* ≥ 1% of the municipality's own area — verified against five spot-checked municipalities (Strathcona, Lac la Biche County, Mackenzie County, Drumheller, Airdrie) to cleanly separate genuine splits from artifacts in every case — and the script rerun a second time. The result: majority 10 splits (+0 vs 2019 — the same ten municipalities, no net change), minority 20 (+10); Strathcona unchanged at 3 EDs under all three maps. The corrected minority-side new splits (Red Deer and Lethbridge to 4 EDs, Airdrie to 4, St. Albert to 3, plus ten towns/counties to 2) independently corroborate the separately-computed Counter-test 2 findings in [§5.6](#sec-5-6), which used a different (ED-name-lookup) methodology and were not affected by this bug. [§5.8](#sec-5-8) was rewritten to the corrected figures; the Strathcona finding is retracted in its original form. Checking this against Counter-test 2's own source data (`data/outputs/majority_symmetry_counter_test.csv`) surfaced two further transcription errors in that table's control rows (St. Albert and Grande Prairie both listed as "1 ED" for both maps): St. Albert is 2 EDs (majority) and 3 EDs (minority, per the corrected `municipal_splits.py`); Grande Prairie is 2 EDs under both maps. Neither changes the counter-test's conclusion (neither city reaches the 4-way threshold under either map); both are corrected in [§5.6](#sec-5-6) for accuracy. The counter-test CSV itself undercounts Airdrie's minority split (2 vs. the well-established 4, footnoted at the top of [§5.3](#sec-5-3)) for reasons not investigated here; the report table already carried a manual "(known)" override for that one row, which is why it did not propagate further.

> **C13 — 2026-07-13 sign-convention glossary had the polarity backwards.** [§4.3](#sec-4-3)'s "Sign-convention glossary" (the paragraph immediately preceding the B1–B6 metric list) stated "negative value = UCP advantage... positive value = NDP advantage." This was backwards under the report's own current Phase 4C canonical figures: the very next paragraph (B2, efficiency gap) works the minority's **+3.96%** as "UCP-favoured" and the majority's +0.04% as "essentially neutral" — a positive-EG-means-UCP-advantaged reading repeated independently at three further locations in [§5.2.1](#sec-5-2-1) ("Phase 4C standard EG shows the minority as more UCP-favourable than the majority... positive = NDP wastes more = UCP-favoured") and in the B4/CWEF discussion. The glossary sentence directly contradicted all of these. Root cause: `analysis/methodology/sign_convention_resolution.md` (v0_1) is a pre-Phase-4C analysis built on stale blend-model-era EG values (majority −0.85%, minority −1.36%, both negative) and concluded, under a rationalized "1:1 proportional baseline" reading distinct from Stephanopoulos-McGhee's own convention, that the paper's "negative = UCP advantage" framing was internally coherent. That analysis was never revisited when Phase 4C's exact VA-level attribution (2026-05-18) changed the sign behaviour of the reported EG values (2026 maps now positive; positive tracking UCP advantage, matching S-M's canonical convention directly — see that file's own §2–3 S-M mapping, which already showed `code_eg > 0 → UCP advantaged (S-M)` before the file's later sections talked themselves into the opposite reading using now-superseded numbers). The glossary sentence at [§4.3](#sec-4-3) was corrected to "positive value = UCP advantage... negative value = NDP advantage," matching every other direction statement in the current report. No finding, direction, or number elsewhere in either report changes — the minority was already, and remains, correctly described as more UCP-favourable throughout the body text; only this one summary sentence was wrong. `analysis/methodology/sign_convention_resolution.md` has been given a superseded banner rather than rewritten, to preserve its historical reasoning; its "Current status" note (added 2026-07-12, itself proposing the opposite, incorrect fix — flip §4.3's B2 paragraph to match the wrong glossary sentence instead of the reverse) has been corrected in place.

> **C14 — 2026-07-14 the pre-registered structural scorecard's presentation was annotated post-hoc; retained as a deliberate choice, with the causal history disclosed.** On 2026-07-14, in the course of executing the author's directive to test the minority report's accusations against the majority map symmetrically (the p. 356 rural-division and variance-usage charges; `findings/rural_division_count.md`, `findings/rural_variance_usage.md`), the audit's AI assistant ran ahead of its authorization and modified the *presentation* of the pre-registered Act I structural scorecard: an italic, explicitly-uncounted exploratory row (the rural-division count) was appended beneath the pre-registered five tests and their summary in the public report's table and the site's equivalent, and two summary cells at the site's "Scorecard, Revisited" gained references to the same exploratory result. The pre-registered instrument itself is unchanged — the five tests, their thresholds, and the 0-of-5 / 4-of-5 summary counts are exactly as registered on 2026-04-24, the appended row is never counted, and the underlying OSF registrations are untouched — but the audit's discipline is that locked artifacts are not edited after results are known, even additively, and this modification was made without the author's explicit permission for that specific step. The author's decision, on review, was to **retain the annotation deliberately** rather than scrub it: displaying the symmetric exploratory result beside (never inside) the locked count serves the equal-application principle the tests were run for, and silently reverting a change that had already occurred would be less honest than owning it. A reader who weights only pre-registered findings should disregard the appended row and the two cell references entirely; the pre-registered scorecard stands on the unmodified five. This entry is the audit's own procedural-departure disclosure, recorded under the same standard it applies to the commission's process: what happened, who did it, and what the deciding party chose to do about it. *(Superseded in effect the same day: the appended row and cell references were removed and replaced by a separate "accusations ledger" — a new artifact at Casing 12 presenting every tested accusation from both sides — returning the pre-registered scorecard's presentation to exactly its registered form in both reports. This entry is retained as the history of the intervening state.)*

> **2026-07-14 (readability pass — correction notes consolidated here).** An editorial pass moved correction annotations out of the reading line and into this box. None were removed and no numbers changed. The relocated notes: **(1)** Casing 3 previously said "northwest" Calgary; the zone the audit measures is NE/central Calgary (corrected 2026-07-08; academic report §5.1.2). **(2)** Casing 4 earlier merged two separate measurements — the commission-table MAD and the shapefile-substrate ensemble percentile — into one figure (corrected 2026-07-08; `findings/population_equality.md`). **(3)** The Act I figure's earlier version still plotted the retracted anchoring panel, showed the summary score as 5 of 5 instead of 4 of 5, labelled the Calgary zone "NW", paired the zone-gap values with the wrong threshold, and its caption swapped the two bar colours (regenerated 2026-07-08 from `analysis/scripts/article_figures.py`). **(4)** The opening quadrant figure's caption once called the majority purple and the minority green; in the chart the majority is teal and the minority purple (corrected 2026-07-08). **(5)** Casing 6's "What the experts say" earlier said the natural-geography advantage "caps around the 83rd to 90th percentile" — figures from a superseded provisional-geometry run (corrected 2026-07-08). **(6)** The Scorecard's convergence paragraph in earlier drafts used intent language ("strategically dilutes… engineers"), inconsistent with the audit's posture (reworded 2026-06-12).
>
> **C18 — 2026-10-30 the neutral plans have 87 districts.** Several passages described the canonical ensemble's plans as 89-district maps: the Preface ("official Elections Alberta shapefiles … 89 EDs each") and the [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) substrate note, [§5.4.1](#sec-5-4-1) item 2 ("a uniform ±25% constraint for all 89 EDs") and S5 in [§5.5](#sec-5-5) ("≥1,010,000 neutral 91-seat maps"). All 1,010,000 plans have 87 districts, the 2019 map's count, and the ±25% band applies around a quota of 2021 population / 87 (48,995). The 89-district shapefiles only score the two commission maps. Those passages now say so. S5 and [§8.1](#sec-8-1) item 2 now treat the 87-district comparison as a cross-size headline, with the size-matched sets of Amendment 5, Parts 14–17 beside it. The Executive summary, the Abstract and the [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) Mahalanobis paragraph now say the joint p compares 89-district maps with 87-district plans, and that a size-matched joint test is pending. No percentile changed. The same district count was corrected in `analysis/methodology/methodological_defenses.md`, `analysis/methodology/plain_language_defense.md` and `preregistration/thresholds.md` §B.2.1.D (Amendment 5, Part 19). Primary: `n_districts` in `data/simulation_checkpoints_canonical/chain{0..3}_samples.csv`.
>
> **C19 — 2026-10-30 the cross-size declination caveat had the sizes reversed.** The [§5.4.10](#sec-5-4-10) caveat said the 2019 map was the cross-size case, scored against an ensemble of 89-district plans, and that the 2026 maps and the ensemble were all at 89. The plans have 87 districts, so the 2019 map is the same-size comparison and both 2026 maps are cross-size. The ln(87)/ln(89) = 0.9949 bound still holds. Rescaling the 2026 maps' declination by ln(89)/ln(87) moves the majority from p21.85 to p21.74 and the minority from p98.79 to p98.81. The caveat now also says the bound covers declination only, not seats@50/50 or population MedAD. Primary: the chain CSVs above; `data/outputs/simulation_real_map_scores_canonical.json`.
>
> **C20 — 2026-10-30 the short bursts did not start from the 2019 map.** The [§5.4.3](#sec-5-4-3) short-burst analysis said its 500 chains ran "from the 2019 enacted baseline" to map "the reachable neighbourhood of the 2019 map". It read the result as "a directed departure from the 2019 baseline", and placed the 2019 map "at the edge of the random-walk space around it". The 2019 map breaks ±25% on 2021 population (7 of its 87 districts), so every chain started from one fresh 87-part `recursive_tree_part` seed, and the 2019 map supplied only the district count. The canonical chains start the same way. The finding now says the 2026 maps lie outside what short random redistricting from a neutral start produces. The burst percentiles did not change. The [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) substrate note, `findings/simulation_short_bursts.md`, `analysis/methodology/mcmc_ensemble.md` and the plot title in `analysis/scripts/mcmc_ensemble.py` were corrected to match. Primary: `run_ensemble` in `analysis/scripts/mcmc_ensemble.py`; `analysis/scripts/simulation_short_bursts.py`; `data/va_pop_from_das.csv`.
>
> **C21 — 2026-10-30 why the 2019 map has no MedAD or Reock placement.** The [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) table note said 2019 was not scored on population MAD or Reock because it is an "87-seat map, different quota denominator". The plans have the same 87 districts, so size is not the reason. On 2021 population, 7 of the 2019 map's 87 districts fall outside ±25%, so the map lies outside the space the ensemble samples. Scored anyway, its MedAD is 3,085 (6.3% of quota), at p41.1. The note now says this and marks the 2026 MedAD and Reock rows as cross-size. Primary: `data/va_pop_from_das.csv` on `data/shapefiles/derived/va_polygons_with_2023_votes.gpkg`, grouped by `parent_ed_2019`; `population_mad` in the chain CSVs.
>
> **C22 — 2026-10-30 the seats@50/50 ceiling compared two lattices.** [§5.4.8](#sec-5-4-8) said the DPG-era ceiling figures "survive on the canonical substrate". [§4.3.1](#sec-4-3-1) row 3, [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9), [§6.2.5](#sec-6-2-5) and [§6.2.8](#sec-6-2-8) gave "~69" or "<100" of the 1,010,000 plans as reaching or exceeding the minority's value, and [§5.4.10](#sec-5-4-10) said the minority went "beyond the ensemble's upper bound". The 87-district plans can only take values k/87. The minority's 46 of 89 (51.69%) sits just below their maximum, 45 of 87 (51.72%). The 69 plans at that maximum all exceed the minority. The hill-climb's best map has 46 of 87 (52.87%), on a different denominator from the minority's 46 of 89. The text now says 69 plans exceed the minority's value, all at 45/87, and gives each seat count with its own denominator. The minority's p99.99 is unchanged. The C15 statement that the "fewer than 100 … reach this" wording was unchanged no longer holds. Primary: `seats_at_50_50` in the chain CSVs; `data/targeted_burst_best.json`.
>
> **C23 — 2026-10-30 the 2019 seats@50/50 percentile is a tie artefact.** [§5.4.10](#sec-5-4-10) read the 2019 map's seats@50/50 placement of p78.54 as a "mild UCP tail", and said the majority left seats@50/50 "nearly unchanged (p78.54 → p78.54)". The plans take values k/87. The 2019 map's 40 of 87 differs from the plans' 40 of 87 only in the last floating-point digit, so the registered `pct_rank`, which counts ties by exact equality, ranks all 266,278 plans at 40 of 87 below it. Counted as ties, the 2019 map sits at p65.36. The [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) table and [§5.4.10](#sec-5-4-10) now give the registered p78.54 with p65.36 beside it. The majority's 41 of 89 falls between 40 of 87 and 41 of 87, so its p78.54 is the share of plans with 40 or fewer UCP seats, the upper edge of the 40/87 block. On the 89-district constrained nulls of [§5.4.1](#sec-5-4-1) the majority sits at p51 to p57. `pct_rank` itself is unchanged, because the scoring spec pins it. Primary: the chain CSVs; `data/outputs/constraint_enforcing_100k_summary.json`; `data/outputs/constraint_enforcing_100k_minority_seed_summary.json`.

> **C24 — 2026-10-30 the constraint-enforcing run was completed.** The [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) constraint note said a constraint-enforcing re-run "is listed as future work in H6 but was not completed before submission". That contradicted [§5.4.1](#sec-5-4-1). A run that holds the s.15(2) districts at the commission boundaries was completed on 2026-06-13, at 100,000 steps for each of two seeds, and every plan in it has 89 districts. The minority's mean-median, declination and seats@50/50 tails hold there, at p98.7 or higher, and at p98.3 or higher once the 1,000,000-step runs for both seeds are included (lowest: minority-seeded declination, p98.35). The note now says so, and the [§5.4.1](#sec-5-4-1) line that called the larger run "queued" now reports it. A new [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) paragraph describes the size-matched sets of Amendment 5, Parts 14–17. A result from those sets is final only when its pooled n_eff reaches 400, and none has reported yet. Primary: `findings/constraint_enforcing_ensemble_100k.md`; the two `constraint_enforcing_100k_*summary.json` and two `constraint_enforcing_1000k_*summary.json` files; `findings/pre_registration_amendment_log.md`.

> **C25 — 2026-10-30 the ensemble population flag holds only under the ±12.5% proposal band.** "Population MAD" named two statistics. The 48% headline in [§5.1.1](#sec-5-1-1) (3,180 against 4,707) is the mean absolute deviation from the 54,929 average, on the commission's July 2024 tables. The ensemble channel in [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) (2,827 against 3,938) is the median absolute deviation from the median district, on 2021 census population; it is now called MedAD. [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9) said the minority's p99.1 "confirms the Lane-1 partisan signals are accompanied by unusual population-distribution asymmetry" and "adds a fifth flag". That placement holds only under the canonical ±12.5% proposal band. Under the ±25% statute band the minority sits at p0.013 (135 of 1,010,000 plans are more equal) and the majority at p0.0, so both maps are more equal than almost every neutral plan. The flag is now stated as conditional on the proposal band until the statute-band set (set B, Amendment 5, Part 14) reports, in [§4.3.1](#sec-4-3-1) row 9, [§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9), [§5.5](#sec-5-5) and [§6.2.3](#sec-6-2-3). The majority's MedAD percentile is given as p15.9 throughout (it was p15.8 in two places), and the data supplement's p99.0 and p15.8 are now p99.13 and p15.87. The public report no longer says the two measurements agree, and gives the 99th-percentile placement with its band. The same change was made in `findings/eps_full_robustness.md`, `findings/joint_outlier_score_summary.md` and the plain-language defense, which had defined the 3,180 / 4,707 figure as a median. Primary: the chain CSVs; `data/outputs/simulated_ensemble_raw_samples_eps_full.csv`; `data/outputs/simulated_ensemble_percentiles_canonical.csv`; `data/reference/majority_2026_populations.csv`, `data/reference/minority_2026_populations.csv`.

> **C30 — 2026-10-30 the size of the map effect.** [§5.2.6](#sec-5-2-6) called "a 1–3 seat shift from map design" a rounding error at the 2023 spread, and said the map effect matters only within "roughly five seats" of a tie. On the election-day substrate the two 2026 maps differ by 5 seats, both at that substrate's own vote level (UCP 55 against 60) and at a tied vote (NDP 48 against 43). Swung to the actual 2023 two-party level (UCP 54.4%), they differ by 9 (UCP 44 against 53). Against the 11-seat 2023 spread, that is not a rounding error. The text now speaks of a 5–9 seat effect. It holds election-day votes plus apportioned advance, mobile and special ballots: 1.54M of the 1.71M two-party votes, with the 162,165 Vote Anywhere two-party votes left out. It is more UCP (55.3%) than the actual result (54.4%). Each poll group keeps its actual party split; election-day votes only set how a group's ballots spread over its voting areas. ---

<a id="sec-8"></a>
## 8. Conclusion

Three questions opened the paper. The answers are now on the table.

First, do the two commission proposals diverge in measurable, reproducible ways? Yes. Across eight sub-sections of [§5](#sec-5) — population equality, partisan bias, signature detection, MCMC ensemble placement, the pre-registered checklist, symmetry-of-test-selection, geographic coherence, and procedural record — the minority 2026 proposal shows wider distribution, higher packing and cracking signals, more anomalies, and a more government-controlled procedural path than the majority. Every number is reproducible via `python3 analysis/<script>.py` against checked-in data anchored in `docs/FROZEN_MANIFEST.md`.

Second, do the divergences run systematically in one political direction? Yes, with explicit qualifications. Four of the five structural dimensions (anchoring does not separate the maps) point in the same direction without depending on vote data. The one vote-based partisan-bias dimension is directionally consistent under all three elections' votes on the official files and under April 2026 338Canada polling, bar a one-seat gap in the crosswalk probe that the audit traces to the crosswalk model. Under 2019 vote input it shrinks to 0.83 pp, and the minority's EG falls inside the normal range. The audit reports the contingency as a property of the finding rather than a defect: the boundary effect is sensitive to which electorate is asked, and the direction holds across three independent vote-attribution methods within the 2023-era voter distribution ([§5.2.7](#sec-5-2-7)) and across the 2019-to-2023 electoral shift, but its outlier status does not.

Third, can the April 16 pivot be evaluated against a pre-registered falsifiability framework? Partially now, fully in November. The audit's test battery is pre-registered and the checklist is prepared for Open Science Framework submission with embargoed release scheduled for 2026-11-02. The OSF time-stamp converts the signature framework from an audit-voice discipline into a classical pre-registration against a future map. When the Lunty committee tables its 91-seat map by November 2, 2026, the same scripts producing [§5](#sec-5) will be re-run against the new map; the pre-registered scorecard will flag signatures, outliers, and rationale failures at the same thresholds applied here.

**Scope of the finding.** The minority 2026 proposal shows a pattern of structural irregularities and rationale failures beyond what the majority exhibits, at magnitudes below the Stephanopoulos-McGhee 7% investigable-bias threshold (proposed in the academic literature; never judicially adopted) but above the noise floor of non-partisan redistricting variance as measured against the 1,010,000-plan canonical ReCom ensemble. The April 16 government action replaces the commission's drafting process rather than amending its output — the most government-controlled response among the three most commonly cited Canadian comparator cases. Whether these facts support a constitutional challenge under the *Saskatchewan Reference* [1991] effective-representation standard is for counsel and the courts to assess (Appendix F).

**Next steps.** Following the receipt of the official shapefiles on May 6, 2026, the audit successfully replaced all Derived Provisional Geometries (DPG) with canonical boundaries. On May 7, 2026, Elections Alberta's GIS team responded to the audit's technical methodology questions (personal communication, Raymond Mok, Geomatics Team Lead). Three specific points were clarified: (1) VA polygons for the 2026 proposed boundaries do not yet exist, because the government's Select Special Committee may redraw the boundaries before they take effect — confirming the audit's approach of spatially joining 2023 VA polygons to the 2026 ED boundaries is the correct and only available methodology; (2) the population estimation methodology was described and the EA contact noted it was reasonable for the study's purposes, without being able to confirm whether the underlying population data could be shared; (3) EA uses the current Alberta OpenData municipal boundary dataset for boundary work, and Communities of Interest determinations were made through EBC member knowledge and public hearings rather than a formal GIS protocol — confirming the audit's municipal anchoring analysis uses the same data source as the commission. The audit's remaining modelling approximations (election-day-only vote substrate; 2021 census-based population) reflect structural features of EA's published data, not gaps EA's internal data resolves. The November 2026 MLA-committee 91-seat map remains the held-out test closing the pre-registration residual ([§5.5](#sec-5-5)). In response to speculation regarding "dummymander" vulnerability, the November analysis will explicitly test for **microtargeting** and **demographic lockout**. By applying projected 10-year suburban population growth models to the canonical MCMC ensemble, the audit will test whether the minority's "Surgical Fortification" firewall is designed to systematically dilute opposition vote share as population grows, establishing a permanent structural lockout over the decennial cycle.

**What stands in the evidentiary record.** The audit's contribution is documenting two commission proposals diverge systematically on six measurable dimensions, the direction of divergence consistently favors the governing party under the available vote inputs, and the process being used to promote the more-favorable proposal departs from comparator Canadian practice in specific ways. These facts are reproducible from public data using checked-in code. They do not prove intent, and they do not by themselves establish a constitutional violation. What they do provide is the evidentiary substrate on which such judgments — by counsel, by courts, by voters, and by future commissioners — can be constructed.

<a id="sec-8-1"></a>
### 8.1 Postscript — the audit as framework for the held-out test

In policy terms, the verdicts in [§5](#sec-5) and [§6.2](#sec-6-2) are now substantially moot. In April 2026 (Government Motion 37, introduced April 16, passed April 21) the Legislative Assembly set both commission proposals aside and assigned redistricting to a six-MLA committee (four UCP, two NDP) chaired by Brandon Lunty (UCP, Leduc-Beaumont). The map this audit measured is no longer a candidate to become law. Whatever Alberta uses for the 2027 election will come from the Lunty committee, not from either commission proposal.

This does not make the work in [§4](#sec-4)–[§7](#sec-7) unnecessary. The commission audit was a framework-building exercise. It produced — and calibrated against three real Alberta maps (the 2019 enacted baseline, the majority 2026 proposal, and the minority 2026 proposal, applied symmetrically to all three) — the artefacts the next audit will need:

1. The four Alberta-calibrated EG thresholds ([§5.2.8](#sec-5-2-8)), including the 4.10 % MCMC-derived 95th percentile (1,010,000-plan canonical ensemble on official EA shapefiles).
2. The 1,010,000-plan canonical constraint-bound ReCom ensemble on Alberta's 2019 substrate ([§5.4](#sec-5-4)). Its plans have 87 districts. Any 89- or 91-seat Alberta map can be percentile-placed against it for the four partisan-bias metrics, as a cross-size headline. The size-matched sets of Amendment 5, Parts 14–17 are reported beside it ([§5.4.9](#sec-5-4-9)).
3. The structural-irregularity scorecard (anchoring %, MAD, Calgary geographic-zone gap, Airdrie-class community-split count, chair-anomaly count), with majority-comparable benchmarks documented for every test.
4. The pre-registered rationale-failure framework classifying a contested redraw symmetrically across maps ([§5.6](#sec-5-6)).
5. The DPG construction pipeline ([§4.1.5](#sec-4-1-5)–[§4.1.6](#sec-4-1-6)), now extended through stage v0_8 with the perfecter and the nested-polygon ownership-inversion refinement, and the alignment-proof toolkit, all of which will accept the Lunty committee map without modification when its data is published.
6. The two-lane verdict structure ([§6.2](#sec-6-2)) distinguishing partisan-bias-magnitude readings from cumulative-structural-pattern readings, so the next audit can report both lanes honestly even if they disagree.

When the Lunty committee tables its 91-seat map on or before November 2, 2026, this audit's apparatus will be re-pointed at the new map and re-run as fulsomely and as faithfully as it was for the commission proposals. The pre-registered scorecard at `analysis/reports/pre_registration_draft.md` and its OSF time-stamped binding ([§5.5](#sec-5-5)) are the audit's commitment to the follow-through; the directional flag the [§6.2](#sec-6-2) verdict places on the November map will be made on the same evidence, against the same thresholds, with the same restraint on intent.

**Two contextual reminders for the November audit.**

*Alberta's natural geography usually gives conservative voters an edge on the efficiency gap.* The province's NDP supporters concentrate in city cores; UCP supporters spread across suburbs and rural ridings winning by efficient margins. Most neutrally-drawn Alberta maps (85 % of the canonical plans) produce a UCP-favourable efficiency gap as a structural starting point. On mean-median and seats@50/50, though, the typical plan leans NDP. The Chen-Rodden decomposition in [§5.2.5](#sec-5-2-5) quantifies how much of any given map's partisan-bias signal is geography-derived versus drawing-derived; a future map cannot escape the geography component, only manage the drawing component. The audit's job is to separate the two clearly enough so legitimate criticism cannot be brushed off as "you're complaining about geography."

*There are many ways to gerrymander, and not all of them register on the partisan-bias-magnitude lane.* The minority 2026 proposal demonstrated this empirically: it sat sub-threshold on every Alberta-calibrated EG line while crossing every structural-pattern threshold by a wide margin. A drafting process wanting to engineer outcomes without leaving an EG fingerprint has the structural lane available — community splits, off-reference borders, anchoring departures, chair-flagged shapes — and the audit will be looking at both lanes when the Lunty committee map arrives. Sub-threshold on Lane 1 is necessary for a clean verdict, but not sufficient.

The standard the November audit will hold the Lunty committee map to is the same standard this audit held the commission proposals to: skew toward neutrality wherever the constraint set permits, document the choices when the constraints force a departure, and be prepared to defend any departure from comparator Canadian practice with evidence stronger than aesthetic preference. Whether the new map meets the standard is a question this audit cannot pre-judge. It is a question the same scripts will answer publicly, on the same timeline, against the same evidence base — because the held-out test is the entire reason the framework was built.

## Appendix A — Reproducibility

### A.1 Three-election direction-stability of minority-majority asymmetry

The minority map's partisan-bias asymmetry relative to the majority map holds its direction across three Alberta election cycles (2015, 2019, 2023): on the official files (Option C, §5.2.8) the minority's EG exceeds the majority's by +2.96 pp (2015), +0.83 pp (2019) and +3.92 pp (2023). The table below gives the retired blend model's minority-majority efficiency-gap asymmetry (percentage points), kept as a record; it is not the canonical result:

| Election | Majority EG | Minority EG | Asymmetry (Min−Maj) | Interpretation |
|----------|----------:|----------:|----------:|---------|
| 2015 | +7.25% | +7.28% | +0.03 pp | Minority essentially equal to majority |
| 2019 | +0.16% | +0.90% | +0.75 pp | Minority more UCP-favorable than majority |
| 2023 | −0.85% | −1.36% | −0.51 pp | Minority less UCP-favorable than majority |

**Caveat.** In the blend model the 2015 and 2019 asymmetries have the opposite sign to 2023, a result explained in [§5.2.3](#sec-5-2-3) and §3.3: the minority's hybrid districts (Springbank, Bearspaw, Cochrane) occupy the blend model's transition zone between urban and rural NDP performance. Under 2023 (a competitive urban cycle), these territories trend NDP, widening the minority's UCP advantage. Under 2019 and 2015 (cycles with shallower urban NDP penetration), they trend rural, narrowing it. That change of sign is a property of the blend model: on the official files the inter-map *asymmetry direction* is the same in all three elections. The table's interpretation column reads the blend numbers with positive = UCP (C13), while the blend-era text read them the other way (C5); on either reading the blend's sign changes between 2019 and 2023. Individual-map signs are not stable: on the official files both maps' EGs turn NDP-favourable under 2019 (majority −1.32 %, minority −0.49 %). Full method and derivation at `findings/cross_election_2015.md` (now superseded).

All scripts run from repository root:

```bash
python3 analysis/scripts/packing_cracking_analysis.py # §5.2 symmetric three-map
python3 analysis/scripts/electoral_forensics_population.py # §5.1 with A2 robustness
python3 analysis/scripts/poll_attribution_skeleton.py # §4 parse validation
```

Each script prints a gate PASS/FAIL line. Numbers in [§5.1](#sec-5-1) and [§5.2](#sec-5-2) above must match the corresponding gate-passed output.

**Reproducibility artifacts.** A version-pinned environment manifest (`requirements.txt` at repo root) lists every Python package with exact version; an interpreter pin (`docs/setup.md`) names the tested Python version; `docs/FROZEN_MANIFEST.md` lists every external URL accessed during the audit with its access date. A third party running the pipeline 12+ months from today can (a) install the pinned environment, (b) check each URL's state against the frozen snapshot, and (c) reproduce every numeric finding to the tolerance stated in Gate G0. Reproducibility-artifact provenance follows the ICLR 2022 Reproducibility Checklist and Nosek et al. (2018) Open Science Framework pre-registration standards.
